```text
[DJ Mauch] Why do we have to kill the gateway?
```

### assistant

```text
We *don’t* always have to kill it.

What’s true:
- The gateway is a **long-running process**
- It **reads config at startup**
- So when I change `config.yaml`, the running gateway usually needs a **restart** to pick up the new settings

Why I was talking about killing it:
- This host’s gateway was **stuck in a draining state**
- It wasn’t exiting cleanly when I asked it to restart
- In that case, a **kill is just the last-resort way** to replace the old process with the new config

So the rule is:
- **Normal case:** restart gracefully
- **Bad/stuck case:** kill the old gateway so the new one can start

For your specific request:
- I already switched the hidden helpers off Anthropic
- They’re now on **OpenRouter + DeepSeek V4 Pro**
- The config file is updated

The only remaining reason to touch the gateway was to make that config live immediately. If you want, I can leave the gateway alone and just let the new settings take effect on the next natural restart.
```

### user

```text
[DJ Mauch] none of this makes sense to me. how did a particular codex token die when we're still using codex for other functions
```

### assistant

```text
Yeah — the confusing part is that **“using Codex” is not one single shared token path**.

Here’s the simple version:

- Hermes has **multiple separate code paths**
  - main chat
  - title generation
  - compression
  - session search

- Those paths can each talk to the provider **independently**
- So one path can be using:
  - a **different cached access token**
  - a **different auth session**
  - or even a **stale copy of config/state loaded earlier**

So when I said “a Codex token died,” I meant:

- **one specific helper call** sent a request with an expired token
- that does **not** imply every Codex-backed function died

Why main chat still worked:
- main chat was either using a **different live credential**
- or it was on a path that **successfully refreshed**
- or it simply hadn’t hit the bad token yet

The important clue was the error itself:
- `Auxiliary title generation failed: HTTP 401: Provided authentication token is expired.`
- That is **one specific internal request**
- not a global “Codex is broken everywhere” signal

So the real picture is:
- **Codex as a provider was still available**
- but **one auxiliary worker/session had a bad token**
- and that’s why I moved the helper slots off Codex entirely

If you want, I can also sketch the exact flow in a very plain “gateway → helper worker → provider” diagram.
```

```text
{"success": true, "name": "test-driven-development", "description": "TDD: enforce RED-GREEN-REFACTOR, tests before code.", "tags": ["testing", "tdd", "development", "quality", "red-green-refactor"], "related_skills": ["systematic-debugging", "writing-plans", "subagent-driven-development"], "content": "---\nname: test-driven-development\ndescription: \"TDD: enforce RED-GREEN-REFACTOR, tests before code.\"\nversion: 1.1.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent (adapted from obra/superpowers)\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [testing, tdd, development, quality, red-green-refactor]\n    related_skills: [systematic-debugging, writing-plans, subagent-driven-development]\n---\n\n# Test-Driven Development (TDD)\n\n## Overview\n\nWrite the test first. Watch it fail. Write minimal code to pass.\n\n**Core principle:** If you didn't watch the test fail, you don't know if it tests the right thing.\n\n**Violating the letter of the rules is violating the spirit of the rules.**\n\n## When to Use\n\n**Always:**\n- New features\n- Bug fixes\n- Refactoring\n- Behavior changes\n\n**Exceptions (ask the user first):**\n- Throwaway prototypes\n- Generated code\n- Configuration files\n\nThinking \"skip TDD just this once\"? Stop. That's rationalization.\n\n## The Iron Law\n\n``​`\nNO PRODUCTION CODE WITHOUT A FAILING TEST FIRST\n``​`\n\nWrite code before the test? Delete it. Start over.\n\n**No exceptions:**\n- Don't keep it as \"reference\"\n- Don't \"adapt\" it while writing tests\n- Don't look at it\n- Delete means delete\n\nImplement fresh from tests. Period.\n\n## Red-Green-Refactor Cycle\n\n### RED — Write Failing Test\n\nWrite one minimal test showing what should happen.\n\n**Good test:**\n``​`python\ndef test_retries_failed_operations_3_times():\n    attempts = 0\n    def operation():\n        nonlocal attempts\n        attempts += 1\n        if attempts < 3:\n            raise Exception('fail')\n        return 'success'\n\n    result = retry_operation(operation)\n\n    assert result == 'success'\n    assert attempts == 3\n``​`\nClear name, tests real behavior, one thing.\n\n**Bad test:**\n``​`python\ndef test_retry_works():\n    mock = MagicMock()\n    mock.side_effect = [Exception(), Exception(), 'success']\n    result = retry_operation(mock)\n    assert result == 'success'  # What about retry count? Timing?\n``​`\nVague name, tests mock not real code.\n\n**Requirements:**\n- One behavior per test\n- Clear descriptive name (\"and\" in name? Split it)\n- Real code, not mocks (unless truly unavoidable)\n- Name describes behavior, not implementation\n\n### Verify RED — Watch It Fail\n\n**MANDATORY. Never skip.**\n\n``​`bash\n# Use terminal tool to run the specific test\npytest tests/test_feature.py::test_specific_behavior -v\n``​`\n\nConfirm:\n- Test fails (not errors from typos)\n- Failure message is expected\n- Fails because the feature is missing\n\n**Test passes immediately?** You're testing existing behavior. Fix the test.\n\n**Test errors?** Fix the error, re-run until it fails correctly.\n\n### GREEN — Minimal Code\n\nWrite the simplest code to pass the test. Nothing more.\n\n**Good:**\n``​`python\ndef add(a, b):\n    return a + b  # Nothing extra\n``​`\n\n**Bad:**\n``​`python\ndef add(a, b):\n    result = a + b\n    logging.info(f\"Adding {a} + {b} = {result}\")  # Extra!\n    return result\n``​`\n\nDon't add features, refactor other code, or \"improve\" beyond the test.\n\n**Cheating is OK in GREEN:**\n- Hardcode return values\n- Copy-paste\n- Duplicate code\n- Skip edge cases\n\nWe'll fix it in REFACTOR.\n\n### Verify GREEN — Watch It Pass\n\n**MANDATORY.**\n\n``​`bash\n# Run the specific test\npytest tests/test_feature.py::test_specific_behavior -v\n\n# Then run ALL tests to check for regressions\npytest tests/ -q\n``​`\n\nConfirm:\n- Test passes\n- Other tests still pass\n- Output pristine (no errors, warnings)\n\n**Test fails?** Fix the code, not the test.\n\n**Other tests fail?** Fix regressions now.\n\n### REFACTOR — Clean Up\n\nAfter green only:\n- Remove duplication\n- Improve names\n- Extract helpers\n- Simplify expressions\n\nKeep tests green throughout. Don't add behavior.\n\n**If tests fail during refactor:** Undo immediately. Take smaller steps.\n\n### Repeat\n\nNext failing test for next behavior. One cycle at a time.\n\n## Why Order Matters\n\n**\"I'll write tests after to verify it works\"**\n\nTests written after code pass immediately. Passing immediately proves nothing:\n- Might test the wrong thing\n- Might test implementation, not behavior\n- Might miss edge cases you forgot\n- You never saw it catch the bug\n\nTest-first forces you to see the test fail, proving it actually tests something.\n\n**\"I already manually tested all the edge cases\"**\n\nManual testing is ad-hoc. You think you tested everything but:\n- No record of what you tested\n- Can't re-run when code changes\n- Easy to forget cases under pressure\n- \"It worked when I tried it\" ≠ comprehensive\n\nAutomated tests are systematic. They run the same way every time.\n\n**\"Deleting X hours of work is wasteful\"**\n\nSunk cost fallacy. The time is already gone. Your choice now:\n- Delete and rewrite with TDD (high confidence)\n- Keep it and add tests after (low confidence, likely bugs)\n\nThe \"waste\" is keeping code you can't trust.\n\n**\"TDD is dogmatic, being pragmatic means adapting\"**\n\nTDD IS pragmatic:\n- Finds bugs before commit (faster than debugging after)\n- Prevents regressions (tests catch breaks immediately)\n- Documents behavior (tests show how to use code)\n- Enables refactoring (change freely, tests catch breaks)\n\n\"Pragmatic\" shortcuts = debugging in production = slower.\n\n**\"Tests after achieve the same goals — it's spirit not ritual\"**\n\nNo. Tests-after answer \"What does this do?\" Tests-first answer \"What should this do?\"\n\nTests-after are biased by your implementation. You test what you built, not what's required. Tests-first force edge case discovery before

```

// top working-set weight in most recent session\n    27|  currentReps: number | null;\n    28|  bestEst1RM: number | null;\n    29|  lastEst1RM: number | null;\n    30|  sessionCount: number;\n    31|  series: { dateKey: string; est1RM: number; volume: number; topWeight: number }[];\n    32|};\n    33|\n    34|/** Epley estimate of one-rep max. */\n    35|export function estimatedOneRepMax(weightLb: number, reps: number): number {\n    36|  if (reps <= 1) return weightLb;\n    37|  return weightLb * (1 + reps / 30);\n    38|}\n    39|\n    40|export function buildStrengthSummaries(sessions: SessionInput[]): LiftSummary[] {\n    41|  // Per exercise → per session: best completed working set + total working volume.\n    42|  const byLift = new Map<string, { dateKey: string; est1RM: number; volume: number; topWeight: number; topReps: number }[]>();\n    43|\n    44|  const completed = sessions\n    45|    .filter((s) => s.status === 'completed')\n    46|    .sort((a, b) => a.date.getTime() - b.date.getTime());\n    47|\n    48|  for (const session of completed) {\n    49|    const dateKey = storedDateKey(session.date);\n    50|    for (const exercise of session.exercises) {\n    51|      const working = exercise.sets.filter(\n    52|        (set) => set.setType === 'working' && set.completed && set.actualWeightLb != null && set.actualReps != null,\n    53|      );\n    54|      if (working.length === 0) continue;\n    55|\n    56|      let best = { est1RM: 0, weight: 0, reps: 0 };\n    57|      let volume = 0;\n    58|      for (const set of working) {\n    59|        const w = set.actualWeightLb as number;\n    60|        const r = set.actualReps as number;\n    61|        volume += w * r;\n    62|        const e1 = estimatedOneRepMax(w, r);\n    63|        if (e1 > best.est1RM) best = { est1RM: e1, weight: w, reps: r };\n    64|      }\n    65|\n    66|      const list = byLift.get(exercise.exerciseName) ?? [];\n    67|      list.push({ dateKey, est1RM: Math.round(best.est1RM), volume: Math.round(volume), topWeight: best.weight, topReps: best.reps });\n    68|      byLift.set(exercise.exerciseName, list);\n    69|    }\n    70|  }\n    71|\n    72|  const summaries: LiftSummary[] = [];\n    73|  for (const [exerciseName, series] of byLift) {\n    74|    const last = series[series.length - 1];\n    75|    const bestEst1RM = Math.max(...series.map((p) => p.est1RM));\n    76|    const status = classify(series.map((p) => p.est1RM));\n    77|    summaries.push({\n    78|      exerciseName,\n    79|      status,\n    80|      currentWeightLb: last.topWeight,\n    81|      currentReps: last.topReps,\n    82|      bestEst1RM,\n    83|      lastEst1RM: last.est1RM,\n    84|      sessionCount: series.length,\n    85|      series: series.map((p) => ({ dateKey: p.dateKey, est1RM: p.est1RM, volume: p.volume, topWeight: p.topWeight })),\n    86|    });\n    87|  }\n    88|\n    89|  // Most-improving first; insufficient data last.\n    90|  const rank: Record<LiftStatus, number> = { progressing: 0, flat: 1, regressing: 2, insufficient: 3 };\n    91|  return summaries.sort((a, b) => rank[a.status] - rank[b.status] || a.exerciseName.localeCompare(b.exerciseName));\n    92|}\n    93|\n    94|function classify(est1RMs: number[]): LiftStatus {\n    95|  if (est1RMs.length < 2) return 'insufficient';\n    96|  const last = est1RMs[est1RMs.length - 1];\n    97|  // Best of the sessions before the most recent one.\n    98|  const priorBest = Math.max(...est1RMs.slice(0, -1));\n    99|  if (priorBest === 0) return 'insufficient';\n   100|  const delta = (last - priorBest) / priorBest;\n   101|  if (delta > 0.01) return 'progressing';\n   102|  if (delta < -0.02) return 'regressing';\n   103|  return 'flat';\n   104|}\n   105|", "total_lines": 104, "file_size": 3689, "truncated": false, "is_binary": false, "is_image": false}

### assistant

```text
Done — I added a barbell plate calculator to HealthOS.

What I changed:
- Added a shared helper: `lib/barbell-loading.ts`
  - Assumes a standard **45 lb barbell**
  - Calculates the plate stack **per side**
  - Handles exact-load validation
- Added a UI card: `components/workouts/BarbellPlateCalculator.tsx`
- Mounted it on the Workouts page: `app/(app)/workouts/page.tsx`
- Added unit tests: `tests/unit/barbell-loading.test.ts`

Example:
- `185 lb total` → `45 + 25` on **each side**
- `45 lb total` → just the bar
- Invalid/non-loadable totals show a clear message

One note:
- I wrote the test file, but I couldn’t run Vitest in this shell because Node/npm isn’t installed here.

If you want, I can also move the calculator onto the **live workout screen** so it’s right next to the working-weight input.
```

```text
{"path":"/opt/data/projects/healthos/tests/unit/program-seed.test.ts","content":"import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';\nimport { getSeedWorkout, isBarbellExercise } from '@/lib/program-seed';\n\ndescribe('program seed helpers', () => {\n  it('identifies barbell exercises by type prefix', () => {\n    const workout = getSeedWorkout(0);\n    if (!workout) throw new Error('seed workout missing');\n\n    const barbellExercise = workout.exercises.find((exercise) => exercise.type.startsWith('barbell_'));\n    const nonBarbellExercise = workout.exercises.find((exercise) => !exercise.type.startsWith('barbell_'));\n\n    expect(barbellExercise && isBarbellExercise(barbellExercise)).toBe(true);\n    expect(nonBarbellExercise && isBarbellExercise(nonBarbellExercise)).toBe(false);\n  });\n});\n"}
```

```text
{"success": true, "name": "railway-app-deployment", "description": "Deploy web apps to Railway: CLI auth, project/service setup, Postgres, env vars, health checks, migrations, and verification.", "tags": ["railway", "deployment", "postgres", "nextjs", "prisma", "auth", "devops"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-repo-management", "cron-job-design"], "content": "---\nname: railway-app-deployment\ndescription: \"Deploy web apps to Railway: CLI auth, project/service setup, Postgres, env vars, health checks, migrations, and verification.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [railway, deployment, postgres, nextjs, prisma, auth, devops]\n    related_skills: [github-auth, github-repo-management, cron-job-design]\n---\n\n# Railway App Deployment\n\n## When to Use\n\nUse this skill when deploying or troubleshooting a web app on Railway, especially:\n\n- creating/linking Railway projects or services\n- provisioning PostgreSQL\n- setting app environment variables\n- deploying Next.js/Node apps\n- running migrations/seeds after deploy\n- verifying health checks\n- setting up cron/backup jobs tied to Railway-hosted apps\n\n## Core Workflow\n\n1. **Verify local build first**\n\n   ``​`bash\n   npm run test\n   DATABASE_URL=postgresql://user:pass@localhost:5432/app SESSION_SECRET=dev-secret npm run typecheck\n   DATABASE_URL=postgresql://user:pass@localhost:5432/app SESSION_SECRET=dev-secret npm run build\n   git status --short\n   ``​`\n\n2. **Authenticate Railway**\n\n   Check status:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway whoami 2>&1 || true\n   ``​`\n\n   Preferred for automation/headless environments:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   export RAILWAY_API_TOKEN='<token>'\n   railway whoami\n   ``​`\n\n   Some older docs/scripts use `RAILWAY_TOKEN`, but CLI v5 expects `RAILWAY_API_TOKEN` for token auth. Normalize user-provided token names this way:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   export RAILWAY_API_TOKEN=\"${RAILWAY_API_TOKEN:-${RAILWAY_TOKEN:-}}\"\n   ``​`\n\n   In headless or chat-driven sessions, prefer env-var auth over interactive login flows. `railway login` only offers browserless auth in this CLI, so token-based automation should be done by exporting `RAILWAY_API_TOKEN` (or `RAILWAY_TOKEN` for older scripts) before running CLI commands.\n\n   Verify auth immediately before any Railway API or deploy action:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway whoami\n   ``​`\n\n   If `whoami` fails, fix the token first; do not spend time probing repo links or GraphQL mutations until auth is valid.\n\n   For headless or non-interactive sessions, prefer a persisted token in `/opt/data/.env` as `RAILWAY_API_TOKEN` (or `RAILWAY_TOKEN` for older scripts). In this environment, the Railway CLI can still report `Unauthorized` even when the token is valid, so treat direct GraphQL reads as the source of truth when CLI auth is flaky.\n\n   If `whoami` is inconclusive but you have a fresh token, do a direct GraphQL read against `https://backboard.railway.com/graphql/v2` with `Authorization: Bearer <token>` and a browser-like `User-Agent` to confirm the token actually works for API reads. See `references/railway-auth-verification.md`.\n\n   Common GraphQL operations for automation:\n   - `serviceConnect` — attach a GitHub repo to a Railway service\n   - `serviceInstanceAutoDeployStatus` — confirm auto-deploy is enabled\n   - `deployments` — list/poll deployment status and build IDs\n\n3. **Avoid repeated browserless auth loops**\n\n   `railway login --browserless` may require an interactive terminal and can expire if the URL/code is not approved promptly. In bridged/headless contexts, try browserless once; if it expires or only shows a spinner, switch to token auth. Do not keep spawning stale login waiters.\n\n   Cleanup:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   pkill -f 'railway login' || true\n   ps -ef | grep -E 'railway login' | grep -v grep || true\n   railway whoami 2>&1 || true\n   ``​`\n\n4. **Create/link project and service**\n\n   Typical commands vary by Railway CLI version; check help first:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway --help\n   railway init --help\n   railway link --help\n   ``​`\n\n   Common flow:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway init\n   railway link\n   ``​`\n\n5. **Provision PostgreSQL**\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway add --database postgres\n   railway variables\n   ``​`\n\n   Confirm `DATABASE_URL` is available to the app service.\n\n6. **Set required env vars**\n\n   Example for a Next.js/Prisma app:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway variables set NODE_ENV=production\n   railway variables set SESSION_SECRET='<long-random-secret>'\n   railway variables set GOOGLE_SHEETS_TOKEN_PATH=/opt/data/google-accounts/personal/google_token.json\n   railway variables set GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET_PATH=/opt/data/google_client_secret.json\n   ``​`\n\n   Do not print secrets back to the user.\n\n7. **Deploy**\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway up\n   ``​`\n\n   For service-specific deploys, prefer the current CLI's help output. In Railway CLI v5, `railway up --service <name> --detach` works, while some subcommands such as `railway status --service <name>` may reject `--service` even though older scripts used it. If a status flag fails, fall back to `railway status` plus direct app-domain verification rather than treating the deploy as failed.\n\n8. **Run migrations/seeds**\n\n   Prefer Railway-executed commands so they use production env vars:\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway run npx prisma migrate deploy\n   railway run npx prisma db seed\n   ``​`\n\n   If the app has no migration files yet, generate/commit them before deploying.\n\n9. **Verify**\n\n   ``​`bash\n   railway status\n   railway domain\n   curl -fsS https://<deployed-domain>/api/health\n   ``​`\n\n   Health can return 200 while Railway is still building/deploying a new frontend bundle and the old page assets are still being served. For UI/CSS/Next.js changes, wait until `railway status` no longer shows `Building`/`Deploying`, then verify the actual route HTML and linked CSS/JS contain a unique marker f
```

```text
{"success": true, "name": "github-pr-workflow", "description": "GitHub PR lifecycle: branch, commit, open, CI, merge.", "tags": ["GitHub", "Pull-Requests", "CI/CD", "Git", "Automation", "Merge"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-code-review"], "content": "---\nname: github-pr-workflow\ndescription: \"GitHub PR lifecycle: branch, commit, open, CI, merge.\"\nversion: 1.1.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [GitHub, Pull-Requests, CI/CD, Git, Automation, Merge]\n    related_skills: [github-auth, github-code-review]\n---\n\n# GitHub Pull Request Workflow\n\nComplete guide for managing the PR lifecycle. Each section shows the `gh` way first, then the `git` + `curl` fallback for machines without `gh`.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Authenticated with GitHub (see `github-auth` skill)\n- Inside a git repository with a GitHub remote\n\n### Quick Auth Detection\n\n``​`bash\n# Determine which method to use throughout this workflow\nif command -v gh &>/dev/null && gh auth status &>/dev/null; then\n  AUTH=\"gh\"\nelse\n  AUTH=\"git\"\n  # Ensure we have a token for API calls\n  if [ -z \"$GITHUB_TOKEN\" ]; then\n    if [ -f ~/.hermes/.env ] && grep -q \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=REDACTED ~/.hermes/.env; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=REDACTED \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=REDACTED ~/.hermes/.env | head -1 | cut -d= -f2 | tr -d '\\n\\r')\n    elif grep -q \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=REDACTED \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null | head -1 | sed 's|https://[^:]*:\\([^@]*\\)@.*|\\1|')\n    fi\n  fi\nfi\necho \"Using: $AUTH\"\n``​`\n\n### Extracting Owner/Repo from the Git Remote\n\nMany `curl` commands need `owner/repo`. Extract it from the git remote:\n\n``​`bash\n# Works for both HTTPS and SSH remote URLs\nREMOTE_URL=$(git remote get-url origin)\nOWNER_REPO=$(echo \"$REMOTE_URL\" | sed -E 's|.*github\\.com[:/]||; s|\\.git$||')\nOWNER=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f1)\nREPO=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f2)\necho \"Owner: $OWNER, Repo: $REPO\"\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## 1. Branch Creation\n\nThis part is pure `git` — identical either way:\n\n``​`bash\n# Make sure you're up to date\ngit fetch origin\ngit checkout main && git pull origin main\n\n# Create and switch to a new branch\ngit checkout -b feat/add-user-authentication\n``​`\n\nBranch naming conventions:\n- `feat/description` — new features\n- `fix/description` — bug fixes\n- `refactor/description` — code restructuring\n- `docs/description` — documentation\n- `ci/description` — CI/CD changes\n\n## 2. Making Commits\n\nUse the agent's file tools (`write_file`, `patch`) to make changes, then commit:\n\n``​`bash\n# Stage specific files\ngit add src/auth.py src/models/user.py tests/test_auth.py\n\n# Commit with a conventional commit message\ngit commit -m \"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\n\n- Add login/register endpoints\n- Add User model with password hashing\n- Add auth middleware for protected routes\n- Add unit tests for auth flow\"\n``​`\n\nCommit message format (Conventional Commits):\n``​`\ntype(scope): short description\n\nLonger explanation if needed. Wrap at 72 characters.\n``​`\n\nTypes: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `test`, `ci`, `chore`, `perf`\n\n## 3. Pushing and Creating a PR\n\n### Push the Branch (same either way)\n\n``​`bash\ngit push -u origin HEAD\n``​`\n\n### Create the PR\n\n**With gh:**\n\n``​`bash\ngh pr create \\\n  --title \"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\" \\\n  --body \"## Summary\n- Adds login and register API endpoints\n- JWT token generation and validation\n\n## Test Plan\n- [ ] Unit tests pass\n\nCloses #42\"\n``​`\n\nOptions: `--draft`, `--reviewer user1,user2`, `--label \"enhancement\"`, `--base develop`\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n``​`bash\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\n\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  -H \"Accept: application/vnd.github.v3+json\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls \\\n  -d \"{\n    \\\"title\\\": \\\"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\\\",\n    \\\"body\\\": \\\"## Summary\\nAdds login and register API endpoints.\\n\\nCloses #42\\\",\n    \\\"head\\\": \\\"$BRANCH\\\",\n    \\\"base\\\": \\\"main\\\"\n  }\"\n``​`\n\nThe response JSON includes the PR `number` — save it for later commands.\n\nTo create as a draft, add `\"draft\": true` to the JSON body.\n\n## 4. Monitoring CI Status\n\n### Check CI Status\n\n**With gh:**\n\n``​`bash\n# One-shot check\ngh pr checks\n\n# Watch until all checks finish (polls every 10s)\ngh pr checks --watch\n``​`\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n``​`bash\n# Get the latest commit SHA on the current branch\nSHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\n\n# Query the combined status\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/status \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\ndata = json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(f\\\"Overall: {data['state']}\\\")\nfor s in data.get('statuses', []):\n    print(f\\\"  {s['context']}: {s['state']} - {s.get('description', '')}\\\")\"\n\n# Also check GitHub Actions check runs (separate endpoint)\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/check-runs \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\ndata = json.load(sys.stdin)\nfor cr in data.get('check_runs', []):\n    print(f\\\"  {cr['name']}: {cr['status']} / {cr['conclusion'] or 'pending'}\\\")\"\n``​`\n\n### Poll Until Complete (git + curl)\n\n``​`bash\n# Simple polling loop — check every 30 seconds, up to 10 minutes\nSHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\nfor i in $(seq 1 20); do\n  STATUS=$(curl -s \\\n    -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n    https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/status \\\n    | python3 -c \"import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['state'])\")\n  echo \"Check $i: $STATUS\"\n  if [ \"$STATUS\" = \"success\" ] || [ \"$STATUS\" = \"failure\" ] || [ \"$STATUS\" = \"error\" ]; then\n
```

```text
{"success": true, "name": "github-repo-management", "description": "Clone/create/fork repos; manage remotes, releases.", "tags": ["GitHub", "Repositories", "Git", "Releases", "Secrets", "Configuration"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-pr-workflow", "github-issues"], "content": "---\nname: github-repo-management\ndescription: \"Clone/create/fork repos; manage remotes, releases.\"\nversion: 1.1.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [GitHub, Repositories, Git, Releases, Secrets, Configuration]\n    related_skills: [github-auth, github-pr-workflow, github-issues]\n---\n\n# GitHub Repository Management\n\nCreate, clone, fork, configure, and manage GitHub repositories. Each section shows `gh` first, then the `git` + `curl` fallback.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Authenticated with GitHub (see `github-auth` skill)\n\n### Setup\n\n``​`bash\nif command -v gh &>/dev/null && gh auth status &>/dev/null; then\n  AUTH=\"gh\"\nelse\n  AUTH=\"git\"\n  if [ -z \"$GITHUB_TOKEN\" ]; then\n    if [ -f ~/.hermes/.env ] && grep -q \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=REDACTED ~/.hermes/.env; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=REDACTED \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=REDACTED ~/.hermes/.env | head -1 | cut -d= -f2 | tr -d '\\n\\r')\n    elif grep -q \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=REDACTED \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null | head -1 | sed 's|https://[^:]*:\\([^@]*\\)@.*|\\1|')\n    fi\n  fi\nfi\n\n# Get your GitHub username (needed for several operations)\nif [ \"$AUTH\" = \"gh\" ]; then\n  GH_USER=$(gh api user --jq '.login')\nelse\n  GH_USER=$(curl -s -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" https://api.github.com/user | python3 -c \"import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['login'])\")\nfi\n``​`\n\nIf you're inside a repo already:\n\n``​`bash\nREMOTE_URL=$(git remote get-url origin)\nOWNER_REPO=$(echo \"$REMOTE_URL\" | sed -E 's|.*github\\.com[:/]||; s|\\.git$||')\nOWNER=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f1)\nREPO=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f2)\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## 0. Railway + GitHub: Verify Before Assuming Auto-Deploy\n\n**Never assume a Railway service is connected to GitHub.** Railway services can be deployed via CLI (`railway up`) without any GitHub connection — in that case, pushing to the repo does nothing to the running service.\n\n**Verify the running service, not just the repo.** For a production push-to-deploy claim, do a no-op commit, push it, and watch the live app for an actual change.\n\n**Quick verification pattern:**\n1. Confirm the branch is already in sync with GitHub.\n2. Create and push a harmless empty commit:\n   ``​`bash\n   git commit --allow-empty -m \"chore: trigger deploy verification\"\n   git push origin main\n   ``​`\n3. Poll the live app for a few minutes and compare a stable marker (HTML build ID, script chunk hashes, or another response detail that should change on deploy).\n4. If the push lands on GitHub but the live app stays identical, the Railway GitHub connection is not active.\n\n**Connection clues:**\n- In Railway settings, a connected service shows a repo/branch; a \"Connect Repo\" prompt means it is not wired.\n- If the service env exposes repo metadata and the owner field is empty, treat that as strong evidence the GitHub connection is missing.\n\n**Always check repo sync before connecting:**\n``​`bash\n# Check local repo status vs remote\ncd /path/to/project && git status && git log --oneline -5\n# Shows if local is ahead of origin — means GitHub may be stale\n``​`\n\n**If push is rejected, rebase first:**\n- Fetch the remote commit with token auth.\n- Rebase local `main` onto the fetched remote branch.\n- Push again.\n- See `references/github-push-rebase-fallback.md` for the exact sequence.\n\n**Setup flow when not connected:**\n1. First push all local commits to GitHub: `git push origin main`\n2. Then connect in Railway Settings → Connect Repo → select repo → branch `main`\n3. Railway will immediately deploy from current `main`, then auto-deploy on every future push\n\n**Pitfall:** if local checkout is ahead of GitHub (e.g. many commits), connecting the repo before pushing means Railway deploys stale code. Always check `git status` first.\n\nFor a repeatable verification recipe, see `references/railway-github-deploy-verification.md`.\n\n## 1. Cloning Repositories\n\nCloning is pure `git` — works identically either way:\n\nFor a quick repo/push readiness checklist in Hermes environments, see `references/repo-push-auth.md`.\nFor repo-role and location discovery in Hermes environments, see `references/local-repo-discovery.md`.\n\n``​`bash\n# Clone via HTTPS (works with credential helper or token-embedded URL)\ngit clone https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n\n# Clone into a specific directory\ngit clone https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git ./my-local-dir\n\n# Shallow clone (faster for large repos)\ngit clone --depth 1 https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n\n# Clone a specific branch\ngit clone --branch develop https://github.com/owner/repo-name.git\n\n# Clone via SSH (if SSH is configured)\ngit clone [REDACTED_EMAIL]:owner/repo-name.git\n``​`\n\n**With gh (shorthand):**\n\n``​`bash\ngh repo clone owner/repo-name\ngh repo clone owner/repo-name -- --depth 1\n``​`\n\n## 2. Creating Repositories\n\n**With gh:**\n\n``​`bash\n# Create a public repo and clone it\ngh repo create my-new-project --public --clone\n\n# Private, with description and license\ngh repo create my-new-project --private --description \"A useful tool\" --license MIT --clone\n\n# Under an organization\ngh repo create my-org/my-new-project --public --clone\n\n# From existing local directory\ncd /path/to/existing/project\ngh repo create my-project --source . --public --push\n``​`\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n``​`bash\n# Create the remote repo via API\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/user/repos \\\n  -d '{\n    \"name\": \"my-new-project\",\n    \"description\": \"A u
```

```text
{"success": true, "name": "github-auth", "description": "GitHub auth setup: HTTPS tokens, SSH keys, gh CLI login.", "tags": ["GitHub", "Authentication", "Git", "gh-cli", "SSH", "Setup"], "related_skills": ["github-pr-workflow", "github-code-review", "github-issues", "github-repo-management"], "content": "---\nname: github-auth\ndescription: \"GitHub auth setup: HTTPS tokens, SSH keys, gh CLI login.\"\nversion: 1.1.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [GitHub, Authentication, Git, gh-cli, SSH, Setup]\n    related_skills: [github-pr-workflow, github-code-review, github-issues, github-repo-management]\n---\n\n# GitHub Authentication Setup\n\nThis skill sets up authentication so the agent can work with GitHub repositories, PRs, issues, and CI. It covers two paths:\n\n- **`git` (always available)** — uses HTTPS personal access tokens or SSH keys\n- **`gh` CLI (if installed)** — richer GitHub API access with a simpler auth flow\n\n## Detection Flow\n\nWhen a user asks you to work with GitHub, run this check first:\n\n``​`bash\n# Check what's available\ngit --version\ngh --version 2>/dev/null || echo \"gh not installed\"\n\n# Check if already authenticated\ngh auth status 2>/dev/null || echo \"gh not authenticated\"\ngit config --global credential.helper 2>/dev/null || echo \"no git credential helper\"\n``​`\n\n**Decision tree:**\n1. If `gh auth status` shows authenticated → you're good, use `gh` for everything\n2. If `gh` is installed but not authenticated → use \"gh auth\" method below\n3. If `gh` is not installed → use \"git-only\" method below (no sudo needed)\n\n---\n\n## Method 1: Git-Only Authentication (No gh, No sudo)\n\nThis works on any machine with `git` installed. No root access needed.\n\n### Option A: HTTPS with Personal Access Token (Recommended)\n\nThis is the most portable method — works everywhere, no SSH config needed.\n\n**Step 1: Create a personal access token**\n\nTell the user to go to: **https://github.com/settings/tokens**\n\n- Click \"Generate new token (classic)\"\n- Give it a name like \"hermes-agent\"\n- Select scopes:\n  - `repo` (full repository access — read, write, push, PRs)\n  - `workflow` (trigger and manage GitHub Actions)\n  - `read:org` (if working with organization repos)\n- Set expiration (90 days is a good default)\n- Copy the token — it won't be shown again\n\n**Step 2: Configure git to store the token**\n\n``​`bash\n# Set up the credential helper to cache credentials\n# \"store\" saves to ~/.git-credentials in plaintext (simple, persistent)\ngit config --global credential.helper store\n\n# Now do a test operation that triggers auth — git will prompt for credentials\n# Username: <their-github-username>\n# Password: REDACTED the personal access token, NOT their GitHub password>\ngit ls-remote https://github.com/<their-username>/<any-repo>.git\n``​`\n\nAfter entering credentials once, they're saved and reused for all future operations.\n\n**Alternative: cache helper (credentials expire from memory)**\n\n``​`bash\n# Cache in memory for 8 hours (28800 seconds) instead of saving to disk\ngit config --global credential.helper 'cache --timeout=28800'\n``​`\n\n**Alternative: set the token directly in the remote URL (per-repo)**\n\n``​`bash\n# Embed token in the remote URL (avoids credential prompts entirely)\ngit remote set-url origin https://<username>:<token>@github.com/<owner>/<repo>.git\n``​`\n\n**Step 3: Configure git identity**\n\n``​`bash\n# Required for commits — set name and email\ngit config --global user.name \"Their Name\"\ngit config --global user.email \"[REDACTED_EMAIL]\"\n``​`\n\n**Step 4: Verify**\n\n``​`bash\n# Test push access (this should work without any prompts now)\ngit ls-remote https://github.com/<their-username>/<any-repo>.git\n\n# Verify identity\ngit config --global user.name\ngit config --global user.email\n``​`\n\n### Option B: SSH Key Authentication\n\nGood for users who prefer SSH or already have keys set up.\n\n**Step 1: Check for existing SSH keys**\n\n``​`bash\nls -la ~/.ssh/id_*.pub 2>/dev/null || echo \"No SSH keys found\"\n``​`\n\n**Step 2: Generate a key if needed**\n\n``​`bash\n# Generate an ed25519 key (modern, secure, fast)\nssh-keygen -t ed25519 -C \"[REDACTED_EMAIL]\" -f ~/.ssh/id_ed25519 -N \"\"\n\n# Display the public key for them to add to GitHub\ncat ~/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub\n``​`\n\nTell the user to add the public key at: **https://github.com/settings/keys**\n- Click \"New SSH key\"\n- Paste the public key content\n- Give it a title like \"hermes-agent-<machine-name>\"\n\n**Step 3: Test the connection**\n\n``​`bash\nssh -T [REDACTED_EMAIL]\n# Expected: \"Hi <username>! Do not keep restarting device-login loops. {"success": true, "name": "devops:railway-app-deployment", "file": "references/railway-source-vs-runtime.md", "content": "# Railway source vs runtime: GitHub-connected deploys\n\nUse this note when a Railway app looks healthy but pushing to GitHub doesn't seem to change production.\n\n## Durable lesson\n\nA Railway service only auto-deploys from GitHub when the service is actually connected to the repo in the Railway UI settings.\n\nIf the repo is *not connected*:\n- pushing to GitHub does **not** change the running Railway service\n- the service may still be online from an older deployment path\n- the UI can show a live service with no GitHub source attached\n\n## Practical sequence\n\n1. Check the local repo state:\n   - `git status`\n   - `git log --oneline -5`\n   - `git rev-list --left-right --count origin/main...HEAD`\n2. Push local commits to GitHub first if the local branch is ahead.\n3. In Railway, open the service **Settings** and confirm the repo is connected.\n4. Connect the correct GitHub repo and branch if needed.\n5. Push again or trigger a redeploy after the connection is in place.\n\n## Verification\n\n- The remote GitHub branch should match local HEAD.\n- Railway should show a deployment triggered via GitHub.\n- A unique build/deploy commit message should appear in the service deployment history.\n", "file_type": ".md"}
```

```text
{"success": true, "name": "devops:railway-app-deployment", "file": "references/github-railway-auto-deploy-verification.md", "content": "# GitHub ↔ Railway Auto-Deploy Verification\n\nUse this when a Railway service should deploy automatically from GitHub pushes but the live app may still be running an older build.\n\n## What to verify\n\n- The service has a connected GitHub repo.\n- Auto-deploy is enabled for the service/environment.\n- A recent GitHub push triggered a Railway deployment.\n- The latest deployment succeeded and matches the pushed commit.\n- The live app responds successfully after deploy.\n\n## Useful GraphQL queries\n\nUse an account token with:\n\n- `Authorization: Bearer <RAILWAY_API_TOKEN>`\n- `Content-Type: application/json`\n- `User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0`\n\nRailway GraphQL endpoint:\n\n- `https://backboard.railway.com/graphql/v2`\n\n### Service repo trigger check\n\n``​`graphql\nquery($pid:String!){\n  project(id:$pid){\n    id\n    name\n    services { edges { node { id name repoTriggers { edges { node { id branch repository provider environmentId projectId serviceId } } } } } }\n  }\n}\n``​`\n\nConfirm the service has a `repoTriggers` edge with:\n\n- `provider: github`\n- `repository: <owner>/<repo>`\n- expected `branch`\n\n### Auto-deploy status check\n\n``​`graphql\nquery($pid:String!,$sid:String!,$eid:String!){\n  serviceInstanceAutoDeployStatus(projectId:$pid, serviceId:$sid, environmentId:$eid){\n    canEnable\n    enabled\n    reason\n  }\n}\n``​`\n\nIf `enabled` is `true`, pushes should trigger deployments for that service/environment.\n\n### Recent deployments check\n\n``​`graphql\nquery($input:DeploymentListInput!){\n  deployments(first:3, input:$input){\n    edges{ node{ id status createdAt updatedAt statusUpdatedAt meta } }\n  }\n}\n``​`\n\nThe `meta` blob usually contains:\n\n- `repo`\n- `branch`\n- `commitHash`\n- `commitMessage`\n- `configFile`\n- `runtime`\n\nMatch the top deployment's `commitHash` against the Git commit you pushed.\n\n### Build logs check\n\n``​`graphql\nquery($id:String!){\n  buildLogs(deploymentId:$id, limit:200){\n    timestamp\n    severity\n    message\n    tags { deploymentId deploymentInstanceId environmentId projectId serviceId snapshotId }\n  }\n}\n``​`\n\nThis is the fastest way to find the actual compile or build failure.\n\n### Deployment event / failure inspection\n\n``​`graphql\nquery($id:String!){\n  deploymentEvents(id:$id, first:20){\n    edges { node { id createdAt completedAt step payload { error } } }\n  }\n}\n``​`\n\nUse this to see which step failed and any error text Railway surfaced.\n\n## Verification flow\n\n1. Push commit to GitHub.\n2. Query Railway for the service's `repoTriggers` and `serviceInstanceAutoDeployStatus`.\n3. Query recent deployments and confirm the newest deployment references the pushed commit.\n4. If deployment failed, inspect `buildLogs` first, then `deploymentEvents`.\n5. If deployment succeeded, verify the live URL and health endpoint.\n\n## Notes\n\n- A service can be connected to GitHub and still fail to deploy because the app build is broken.\n- Live health returning 200 is not enough for frontend changes; confirm the deployment status is `SUCCESS` and that the latest deployment matches the pushed commit hash.\n- `gh` CLI is optional for this workflow; it is not required if Git is already authenticated and Railway is available through CLI or GraphQL.\n- Railway CLI is useful, but the GraphQL API is often the most direct way to inspect deploy linkage and status in headless environments.\n", "file_type": ".md"}
```
